# Consolidation: one game loop on the per-TU ports (plan, 2026-10-30)

Status: design. Nothing here is implemented yet. Inventories were measured on `rust-port` at `98c63e58e`.
The scripts that produced them are described in section 5, so any agent can regenerate them.

## 0. The problem in one paragraph

`melee golden` runs `rust/melee/src/vs_match.rs` plus `vs_match/*.rs` (about 15.7k lines). That code is a hand-assembled
driver. Its `Sim::step` calls `fighters()`, `stage_anim()`, `effects_update()`, the particle system and the HUD in a fixed
order. It keeps its own `Player`/`VsMatch` state and reaches the per-TU ports (`melee_*/src/tu/`) only for some
leaves. Many C functions are re-implemented in it, some under C names and some under invented names (`dash_enter`,
`guard_iasa`, ...). Items and effects are models that keep only the hashed fields. Meanwhile the TUs already
hold faithful ports of the whole call graph the C trace shows: 2577 of the 2625 functions in
`docs/rust-port/trace/pad_falcon_fox_fd.csv` are `ported+verified`, including `HSD_GObj_RunProcs`, `Fighter_Create`, every
`Fighter_8006*` phase, `Item_*`, `Ground_*`, `efLib_*`, `Camera_*` and `gm_801A4D34` (the scene loop). These ports are
not wired together. They reach each other through about 870 `ext_fns!` hook tables (794 hook defaults are still
`todo!()`) and through `extern "C"` blocks. On top of that, 2353 C functions have more than one Rust body.

The goal is that a golden run is the C program's own call sequence: `gm_801A4D34`, then `HSD_GObj_RunProcs` over the real
GObj p_link lists, running the procs that the real `Fighter_Create` / `Item_Create` / `Ground_*` / `efLib_Create` registered,
with data instantiated from the disc by the real loaders. Every byte of every migrated subsystem must equal the C build.

## 1. Target architecture

### 1.1 Crates and ownership

| layer | crate | owns |
|---|---|---|
| leaves | `melee_runtime`, `melee_world`, `melee_fighter`, `melee_chara`, `melee_items`, `melee_modes` | Game logic, only in `src/tu/<cfile>.rs`: one Rust module per C TU, with the C's globals as the module's statics (as `tu/gobj_native.rs` does now). No crate calls another crate's logic except through the link layer (1.2). |
| link | **new `melee_link`** (depends on every leaf crate) | `install()`: sets every `ext_fns!` hook and every `HOOK` static to the canonical TU function, from a **generated** table (`melee_link/src/gen_table.rs`, made by `tools/rust-port/link/gen_link.py` from the canonical-home table in section 3). The crate contains no hand-written logic. |
| engine | **new `melee_engine`** (depends on `melee_link`) | The host side the C gets from the OS and dolphin SDK: the heap (`HSD_MemAlloc` / `OSAlloc` arenas), the DVD/archive host (1.4), pad feed into `HSD_PadRenewStatus`, `OSGetTick`, the audio sink (`melee_audio::sim`), the render sink (no-op or `melee_gx`). Plus `Engine::boot(scenario)` / `Engine::tick()` / `Engine::state()`. |
| bin | `melee` | CLI, disc, golden CSV writer, `diff`, snapshots, viewers, netplay. No game logic (lint, section 4). |

`vs_match` becomes a second, legacy `Sim` behind `--engine legacy` until the cutover step, and is then deleted.

### 1.2 Linking: one mechanism, generated

Today there are two ways for a TU to call another TU:
- `extern "C" { fn X(); }` blocks, resolved by the linker against a `#[no_mangle]` definition;
- `ext_fns!` / `HOOK` statics with a default body, often `todo!()` or a copied local implementation.

Target: **`extern "C"` declarations plus exactly one `#[no_mangle] pub unsafe extern "C" fn` per C function**, in
its canonical TU. A duplicate definition then fails at link time, which gives a free dedupe check. Rules:
- A C function whose Rust signature is not C-ABI (it takes `&mut Fighter`) gets a thin `#[no_mangle]` C-ABI shim
  next to it in the same TU file. The shim is generated by `gen_link.py`, and the Rust-typed body stays.
- `ext_fns!` stays only for **true host seams**: `lbAudioAx_*` (audio sink), `HSD_DVD*`/`DVDRead` (disc), `GXDraw*`
  (render), `OSReport`, script/mod/Geno hooks (`ScriptGame_*`, `Mex_*`, `Geno_*`, TARGET_PC extensions). For each seam,
  `melee_engine` installs the host implementation. The list is `melee_link/seams.txt`.
- Every other `ext_fns!` entry is converted to an `extern "C"` declaration by `gen_link.py --convert <file>`. Its local
  default body is deleted, so this conversion is also how the 1667 TU-to-TU copies in the dedupe list get deleted.

Until a TU has been converted, `melee_link::install()` sets its hook pointers to the canonical function, so behaviour is
the same either way.

### 1.3 The loop and GObj dispatch: exactly the C's

- **One GObj system.** `melee_runtime::tu::gobj_native` is the one system: the C globals `HSD_GObjPLinkHead`,
  `plinklow_gobjs`, `HSD_GObj_ProcList` and the GC-layout `HSD_GObj` / `HSD_GObjProc` records. The id-based model
  `melee_runtime::gobj::GObjSystem` stays a test oracle only (it is re-exported today by `tu/gobjproc.rs`, and that
  re-export moves to `tests/`).
- **Registration** is what the C does: `GObj_Create(class, p_link, prio)`, `HSD_GObj_SetupProc(gobj, func, s_link)`,
  `GObj_SetupGXLink`, `GObj_InitUserData`. `func` is the canonical TU function's C-ABI pointer (for example
  `Fighter_8006A1BC`, `Item_80268E5C`'s proc set, `grBattle_*`, `efLib_*`, `Camera_*`, `ifStatus_*`). Nothing in the engine
  or the bin registers a proc.
- **Dispatch** is `HSD_GObj_RunProcs` (gobj_native, ported and C-oracle-verified). It walks the s_link lists in
  p_link and priority order, with the delayed-remove and delayed-reprioritize rules (`DelayedProcInfo`).
- **The frame** is `melee_modes::tu::gmscene::gm_801A4D34(on_frame, info)`, the C scene loop. It calls the scene's
  `on_frame`, then `HSD_GObj_RunProcs`, then the camera refresh, then render (`HSD_GObj_80390FC0` with the render sink).
  `Engine::tick()` runs exactly one iteration. To do that, the loop body is factored into `gm_801A4D34_tick()` in the
  same TU (a TARGET_PC split like `gw_*`, with a comment naming the C lines). `gm_801A4D34` itself becomes
  `loop { tick }`.
- **The scene** is the real VS boot: the `gmvsmelee` / `gm_16f1` minor scene `OnLoad` (`StartMelee` path), then
  `Stage_*` init, `Player_InitAllPlayers`, `Player_80032070` and `Fighter_Create` per slot, then `Item` / `ef` / `cm` /
  `if` init. A scenario is a `StartMeleeData` built from `cfg.rs`, not a hand-built `VsMatch::new`.
- **Determinism hooks** that exist today (`TRACE_FRAME`, `MELEE_GOLDEN_RAND`) move into `melee_engine` as an
  observer. `HSD_Randi` is the real `melee_runtime::tu::random` global, with no `with_rand` pointer juggling.

### 1.4 Data from the disc

- `melee_engine::dvd` implements the dolphin DVD seam (`DVDOpen` / `DVDReadAsync` / `DVDGetLength`) on `melee::disc`.
  The game then runs `lbFile` / `lbArchive_LoadSymbols` / `HSD_ArchiveParse` itself.
- **Instantiation.** An archive's data is GC big-endian with 32-bit offsets, but TU code reads native structs. The
  archive host keeps two images per file:
  1. the **raw image**, relocated as the C does it, at a sub-4-GiB address (an arena allocated with
     `VirtualAlloc`/`mmap` below 4 GiB). Byte-level C code that reads raw `u8` uses this image;
  2. the **native image**, made by walking the archive's root symbols with their melee_types `GcDecode` record types
     (the `crate::dat` relocation set plus `GcDecoder`, as `melee_runtime/src/dat.rs` does now). It is native
     little-endian, and pointers inside it point into the same native image.

  `HSD_ArchiveGetPublicAddress(name)` returns the native-image pointer. The symbol-name-to-record-type map is
  `melee_engine/src/dat_types.rs`, a generated table built from the `lbArchive_LoadSymbols` call sites. An unknown symbol
  is a hard stop that names the symbol.
- `melee/src/fighter_data.rs`, `stage.rs`, `movetab.rs` and `skeleton.rs` stop being data sources. Each becomes a test
  that compares its decode with the engine's native image, and is then deleted with vs_match.

### 1.5 What the golden reads

`frame_words()` reads the real objects: the Fighter user_data of every gobj on the fighter p_link
(`HSD_GObjPLinkHead[8]`), the item p_link list, `HSD_Rand` seed and the stocks in `gmMainLib`. This is the NHV1 recipe
from `docs/rust-port/neutral-hash.md`, applied to engine memory rather than to `Player`.

### 1.6 Snapshots, rollback, diff

The engine state is the heap arenas plus the TU statics. `Engine::state()` serializes the arenas (GC layout through
`GcLayout`) and a generated list of TU statics. That one serializer then serves several jobs:
- loading a C snapshot (`melee golden --from-snapshot`, which becomes a plain overlay of identical layouts);
- rollback save and load;
- `melee diff`, which compares C and Rust objects field by field with no "model state" section.

## 2. Migration steps (one agent session each; every step ends with the four goldens still matching on `--engine legacy`)

Gate at the end of every step: `tools/rust-port/gate.sh` (section 3.3). It checks four things:
1. the four legacy goldens are at or above their recorded frame counts;
2. the new engine's frame counts are at or above the previous step's counts (in `docs/rust-port/engine_progress.tsv`);
3. the diff baseline of every subsystem marked migrated has not grown;
4. the lint introduces no new violations.

From step 7 the engine path is the one being moved forward.

Merges into the same TU file are serialized. One exception: a function that a later engine step will exercise moves
first and is verified by its C oracle.

`fmsubs_neg` (clang forms fmuladd(a,c,-b)); fixed. `melee golden` caps `pad_falcon_fox_fd` / `pad_marth_sheik_bf` at 1802 frames
(marth_sheik used to run to the caller's timeout). Legacy counts recorded in `docs/rust-port/engine_progress.tsv`:
cpu_fox_falco_dl matches 0..369 (hash divergence from 370, stop at 414 `ftCo_AttackLw3`; STATUS.md's 323 is stale and the
divergence at 370 predates step 0), cpu_peach 120. Diff baseline unchanged (144 entries).

Step 1 result: `melee_core::ct!("name")` is the first statement of 20708 top-level pub fns in `rust/*/src/tu/**` (inserted by
`tools/rust-port/trace/insert_ct.py`, idempotent, `--remove`/`--check`); it expands to nothing without the `melee_core/calltrace` feature
(`cargo build -p melee --release --features calltrace`; the cfg is on the macro definition, so no crate needs its own feature). `melee golden
--calltrace OUT [--calltrace-frames A-B]` writes `frame,fn,seq` (frame -1 = static init). `pc/platform/gw_calltrace.c` gained
`MELEE_CALLTRACE_SEQ=<file>` + `MELEE_CALLTRACE_FRAMES=a-b` (ordered per-match-frame list of rvas); `seqdiff.py --c --map --rust` reports the first divergent
call (case-insensitive names, scope = functions the Rust side logged unless `--all`, `--filter`, `--ignore-inlined`). The Rust side was
run (pad_falcon_fox_fd 0..130: 3475 calls, legacy path reaches only ftcoll/lbColl leaves). The C side and seqdiff against real C output are NOT

## 3. Inventories

### 3.1 Stand-ins and models in vs_match (what each fakes, what replaces it)

| file | fakes / models | replaced by (canonical TU) |
|---|---|---|
| `vs_match.rs` `VsMatch`, `Player`, `*Vars`, `Hitbox`, `EntryCommon` | The whole object model: per-player state outside `Fighter`, its own joint trees, effect list, item list and stage tables. | Real `HSD_GObj` + `Fighter` / `Item_GObj` / `Ground` user_data (`gobj_native`, `ftdata`, `item.rs`, `ground.rs`). |
| `vs_match.rs` `Sim::step` / `fighters()` / `stage_anim()` / `effects_update()` | Fixed phase order in place of `HSD_GObj_RunProcs`. | `gmscene::gm_801A4D34` and `gobj_native::HSD_GObj_RunProcs`. |
| `vs_match.rs` motion-state code (`change_motion_state*`, `load_row`, `set_anim_rate`, `proc_update_integrate`) | `Fighter_ChangeMotionState`, `ftAnim_*` and `Fighter_UpdatePosition` on its `AnimRow` / `FigaTree` cache. | `melee_fighter::tu::fighter` (`Fighter_ChangeMotionState`, `Fighter_8006A1BC`...), `tu::ftanim`. |
| `vs_match.rs` state callbacks (`wait_iasa`, `walk_*`, `turn_enter_basic`, `jump_*`, `kneebend_*`, `fall_*`, `landing_*`, `guard_*`, `ottotto_*`, `attack*_enter/anim/iasa`, `catch_*`, `special_checks*`, `ftco_8009*` guard helpers, `ftco_800cb024`, `ftco_800cb870`, `ftcommon_8007*`, `ft_80083e64`, `ft_80081a00`, `ft_800831cc`, `ftcommon_8008031c`, `ftaction_80072cd8`, `ft_8008a2bc`) | ftCommon states re-implemented. | `tu/ftCo_Wait.rs`, `ftCo_Walk.rs`, `ftCo_Turn.rs`, `ftCo_Jump.rs`, `ftCo_JumpAerial.rs`, `ftCo_KneeBend.rs`, `ftCo_Fall.rs`, `ftCo_Landing.rs`, `ftCo_Guard.rs`, `ftCo_Ottotto.rs`, `ftCo_Attack1.rs`, `ftCo_Attack100.rs`, `ftCo_AttackS3.rs`, `ftCo_AttackS4.rs`, `ftCo_Catch.rs`, `ft_0819.rs`, `ft_081b.rs`, `ftcommon.rs`, `ftaction.rs`, `ft_0877`-area TUs. |
| `vs_match.rs` `ftms_speciallw_*`, `ftca_speciallw_*` | Marth Counter and Falcon Kick. | `melee_chara::tu::ftmarsspeciallw`, `ftcaptainspeciallw`. |
| `vs_match.rs` `hit_guard` | Blocks the real hit code: it stops the run when an unmodelled hit would happen. | `melee_fighter::tu::ftcoll` through `Fighter_8006CB94` / `Fighter_ProcessHit_8006D1EC` procs. |
| `vs_match.rs` `ef_*`, `effect_*`, `dptcl_spawns`, `Effect` / `EffectTrack` / `EffectRoot` / `DptclAnim` | Effects kept as a lifetime plus particle tracks, with no GObj or JObj. | `melee_world::tu::eflib` / `efasync` / `efsync` with real `HSD_JObj` + `HSD_AObj`. `melee_runtime::tu::{particle,generator,psappsrt}`. |
| `vs_match.rs` `blast_check`, `dead_*`, `BlastZone` | KO detection. | `ftCo_DeadDown`-family TU (`ft_0d27.rs` / `ftco_0d72.rs`), `Stage` blast points (`ground.rs`). |
| `vs_match.rs` `item_spawner_init`, `greens_init`, `greens_sync_joints` | Item spawner seed draw and Green Greens setup. | `melee_items::tu::itspawn` (`fn_8026C88C`), `melee_world::tu::grgreens`. |
| `vs_match.rs` `player_nudge`, `hurt_extents`, `ecb_joints`, `refresh_fighter_jobj` | Fighter push-apart, hurt capsules, ECB from its skeleton. | `tu/fighter.rs` (`Fighter_8006CFE0`-area), `ftcoll.rs`, `ftparts.rs`, `melee_runtime::tu::jobj`. |
| `vs_match.rs` HUD block in `step` (`p.hud.step`) | ifStatus shake and damage. | `melee_world::tu::ifstatus` procs (`ifStatus_802F5B48`, `802F4EDC`, `802F4B84`). |
| `vs_match/attackair.rs` | `ftCo_AttackAir` entry, anim, IASA, phys and coll, and LandingAir without the L-cancel lag branch. | `melee_fighter::tu::ftCo_AttackAir`, `ftCo_LandingAir`. |
| `vs_match/cpu.rs` (2941 lines) | A second hand port of the CPU AI (`ftCo_800B3900`, `ftco_800a*` / `800b*`) on `Cx`. | `melee_fighter::tu::ftCo_0A01_ai` and `ftcpuattack` (both ported), `ftcmdscript`. |
| `vs_match/damage.rs` | Thread-local `with_sim` routing of `Fighter_ChangeMotionState` / `ftAnim_8006EBA4` hooks into `Player`. | Direct `extern "C"` calls into `tu::fighter` / `tu::ftanim` (link layer). |
| `vs_match/dashrun.rs` | Dash and Run with no smash turn, RunBrake or button interrupts. | `tu/ftCo_Dash.rs`, `ftCo_Run.rs`, `ftCo_RunBrake.rs`, `ftCo_TurnRun.rs`. |
| `vs_match/fighthit.rs` | Pseudo joints (`JOINTS`, leaked `parts`), the `lb_8000B1CC` hook mapped to its skeleton, and stand-in GObjs. | Real fighter JObj tree (`ftparts`, `Fighter_Create`), `melee_runtime::tu::lb_00B0::lb_8000B1CC`. |
| `vs_match/fox.rs` | Blaster gun and lasers as kind, msid, pos and vel. No model animation, muzzle effect, ground collision or hit. | `melee_chara::tu::ftfoxspecialn`, `melee_items::tu::itfoxblaster`, `itfoxlaser`, `ithitbox`, `itcoll`. |
| `vs_match/foxlw.rs` | Reflector without reflect bone, effect hitlag callbacks or pass-through. | `melee_chara::tu::ftfoxspeciallw`. |
| `vs_match/greens.rs`, `greens_blocks.rs` | Whispy wind and the block state machines, with no wind force, apples or block hits. | `melee_world::tu::grgreens`, `melee_items::tu::itwhispyapple`, `ftlib` wind (`lb_80011A50`). |
| `vs_match/hit.rs` | Item-vs-fighter hits for lasers only. | `melee_fighter::tu::ftcoll` (`ftColl_8007925C`, `8007AB48`, `8007A06C`), `tu::fighter` (`Fighter_UnkTakeDamage_8006CC30`). |
| `vs_match/rebirth.rs` | Respawn with no platform model, camera box, colour animation or intangibility. | `tu/ft_0d4d.rs`, `ftCo_*Rebirth*`, `melee_modes::tu::gm_1601` (`gm_80167320`, `fn_8016719C`), `tu/player.rs` (`Player_80032070`). |
| `vs_match/rollback.rs` | Save and load of the `VsMatch` components. | `Engine::state()` (step 17). |
| `vs_match/samus.rs` | Missile, ground states only. | `melee_chara::tu::ftsamusspecials` (canonical home **missing**: create from the C), `ftsamusspecialn`, `melee_items::tu::itsamusmissile`. |
| `vs_match/sheik.rs` | Needle Storm and needles as hashed fields only, with no ground-hit states, SFX or bundle model. | `melee_chara::tu::ftseakspecialn`, `melee_items::tu::itseakneedleheld`, `itseakneedlethrown`. |
| `vs_match/snapshot*.rs` | Overlays C state onto the model, and recovers effect identity from tree shapes. | Layout-identical overlay of engine arenas (step 17). |
| `vs_match/sound.rs` | `with_rand` pointer for `HSD_Randi` inside `ft_PlaySFX`. | Real `melee_runtime::tu::random` global, `lbAudioAx` seam. |
| `vs_match/story.rs` | Randall and Shy Guy timers, with no Shy Guys, Randall collision or map animations. | `melee_world::tu::grstory`, `melee_items::tu::itheiho`, `ground.rs` (`Ground_UpdateMapColl`). |
| `melee/src/{fighter_data,movetab,skeleton,dynamics,effect,colanim,cm_drive,stage,stage_anim}.rs` | Data loaders and simulation helpers feeding vs_match. | The engine archive host (1.4), `tu/ftdata`, `ftdynamics`, `ftcolanim`, `cmcamera`, `ground`. |

C-named functions defined in vs_match **and** elsewhere: 112 bodies (`V:` entries in 3.2). Invented-name
re-implementations (the state callbacks above) are not in the C-name count. They go away with step 19.

### 3.2 Functions with more than one body (dedupe list)

**Size: 2353 C functions with 2 or more Rust bodies, 3404 non-canonical bodies to delete.**

| where the extra bodies are | bodies |
|---|---:|
| `T`: another `tu/` file (local copy of a callee) | 1667 |
| `N`: a non-tu module (`melee_fighter/src/ftcommon*.rs`, `melee_world/src/gr/`, `melee_items/src/itkinds*.rs`, `melee_modes/src/gm_r*.rs`, `tu_ext.rs`, `adapt.rs`, `stubs.rs`, ...) | 1499 |
| `V`: `melee/src/vs_match*` | 112 |
| `C`: `melee_chara/src/chars/*.rs` | 94 |
| `R`: `melee_chara/src/chars/rN/*.rs` | 32 |

Rule for the canonical home: **the `tu/` module named after the C file that defines the function**, in the crate that
owns that C directory. The mapping is `ft/kinds/<Char>` to `melee_chara`, other `ft/` and `pl/` to `melee_fighter`,
`it/ ty/ vi/` to `melee_items`, `gr/ mp/ cm/ ef/ if/` to `melee_world`, `gm/ mn/` to `melee_modes`, and `lb/ db/ sysdolphin/`
to `melee_runtime`. The C file is found by scanning `src/melee`, `src/sysdolphin` and `pc/gameworld` for the function's
definition. Static functions that share a name across C files (107 names) are excluded: each C file legitimately has
its own. Header `static inline`s go in `<crate>/src/tu/inl_<header>.rs`, with one body. Six canonical files do not
exist yet (`NEW` below). They are created with the first body moved in.

The full list is in Appendix A: grouped by canonical file, then function, then `[category:path of each extra body]`.
Paths drop `melee_`, `/src` and `/tu`.

## 4. The stricter correctness gate

### 4.1 Definition

A step passes when **both** of these hold:
1. **Goldens.** All four scenarios match their CSV from frame 0 for at least the recorded count. The counts are in
   `docs/rust-port/engine_progress.tsv`, with columns `scenario`, `legacy`, `engine`, `step` and `date`.
2. **Field diff.** `melee diff --all` at the sampled frames shows **zero** differing fields in every subsystem that
   `docs/rust-port/migrated.txt` lists, apart from those listed in `tools/rust-port/diff/baseline/<subsystem>.txt`.
   - Baseline files may only shrink. The gate fails if a line is added.
   - A migrated subsystem starts with an empty baseline unless the step's notes justify each line, for example
     "C reads uninitialised stack; value is garbage". Hashed fields are never allowed in a baseline.

Subsystems and their diff instances:

| subsystem | instances |
|---|---|
| `gobj` | p_link lists, proc lists, order |
| `fighter` | Fighter records |
| `item` | Item records |
| `stage` | Ground and grXX vars |
| `mp` | collision lines |
| `camera` | `cm` globals |
| `effect` | ef objects |
| `particle` | ps / generator |
| `hud` | if objects |
| `rng` | `HSD_Rand` |
| `globals` | `gmMainLib`, `Player` statics |

### 4.2 Sampled frames

| scenario | sampled frames |
|---|---|
| `pad_falcon_fox_fd` | 0, 1, 60, 121, 300, 600, 1200, 1800 |
| `pad_marth_sheik_bf` | 0, 121, 450, 900, 1800 |
| `cpu_fox_falco_dl` | 0, 100, 200, 322, and the last engine frame |
| `cpu_peach_samus_ys_items` | 0, 60, 119 |

Each frame is capped at the engine's current count for that scenario. The list lives in `tools/rust-port/diff/frames.tsv`.

### 4.3 How to run it

```
set -a; . "<workspace>/.env"; set +a                       # GW_ISO_VANILLA, never printed
export GW_BUILD_ROOT="<workspace>/_build/agents/<your-name>"  # private C build root with melee-pc.exe/.map
# 1. C snapshots for the sampled frames (once per C build; ~13 s per frame)
for s in pad_falcon_fox_fd pad_marth_sheik_bf cpu_fox_falco_dl cpu_peach_samus_ys_items; do
  bash tools/rust-port/snap/dump.sh $s "$(awk -v s=$s '$1==s{print $2}' tools/rust-port/diff/frames.tsv)"; done
# 2. the gate (builds release once, runs goldens + diffs, compares counts and baselines)
bash tools/rust-port/gate.sh [--engine tu|legacy] [--update-progress]
```

`gate.sh` does the following, and any failure exits non-zero:
1. `cd rust && CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0 CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0 CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 cargo build -p melee --release`.
2. For each scenario: `melee golden --engine E --scenario S [--idle-pads] --out $T/S.csv --max-frames 1801`, then
   `compare_golden.py docs/rust-port/golden/S.csv $T/S.csv --allow-short --print-count`. It fails if the count is
   below the `engine_progress.tsv` value for E.
3. For each sampled frame up to that count: `melee diff --engine E --scenario S --frame N --all --subsystems
   $(cat docs/rust-port/migrated.txt) --baseline-check tools/rust-port/diff/baseline/ --max 0`. This exits 1 if any field
   outside the baseline differs, and also if a baseline line is never hit, which means the line can be removed (printed
   as `STALE`).
4. `python tools/rust-port/lint_logic.py` (section 5) against `lint_baseline.txt`.
5. With `--update-progress`: write the new counts to `engine_progress.tsv`, only when they did not go down.

`melee diff` additions this needs: `--engine`, `--subsystems`, `--baseline-check DIR`, and `--max 0` meaning "exit
status only". Detail goes in notes/diff.md when the step lands.

### 4.4 Call-sequence check (from step 1)

`melee golden --engine tu --calltrace $T/r.ct` and the C `MELEE_CALLTRACE` run of the same scenario produce
per-frame ordered call lists. `python tools/rust-port/trace/seqdiff.py --c c.ct --rust r.ct --frames 0-<count>` prints
the first frame and position where the sequences differ. A migrated subsystem's functions must have zero sequence
differences. Inlined-in-C helpers are filtered with `--ignore-inlined`, using the map from `calltrace.py`.

## 5. Lint: game logic only in tu/

`tools/rust-port/lint_logic.py`, run in `.github/workflows/build.yml` after `cargo build`.

**Input.**
- Every `.rs` under `rust/*/src`, except `raw/`, `c2r/` (c2rust reference, never compiled into logic), `gen.rs` and
  `tests/`.
- The C definition index: every function defined in `src/melee/**/*.c`, `src/sysdolphin/**/*.c` and
  `pc/gameworld/*.c`, minus static names that are defined in more than one file.

**Rules.** Each rule is reported as `path:line rule name`.
1. **R1 logic outside tu.** A `fn` whose name (case-insensitive) is a C-defined function, with a non-trivial body
   (not `todo!` / `unimplemented!`, and not a one-expression forwarder to `crate::tu::...`), in a file that is not under
   `*/src/tu/` and not allowlisted.
2. **R2 duplicate body.** The same C name has a body in more than one file. The canonical one is the `tu/` file named
   after the C file (section 3.2), and every other body is a violation.
3. **R3 wrong home.** A C function's only body is in a `tu/` file other than its C file's TU.
4. **R4 hook default logic.** An `ext_fns!` / `HOOK` default that is more than a `todo!` or a call into `crate::tu` /
   `melee_link`, for a function that is not in `melee_link/seams.txt`.
5. **R5 driver logic.** In allowlisted glue files, a fn that writes a `Fighter` / `Item_GObj` / `Ground` field or calls
   `HSD_Randi` / `HSD_Randf`, other than through a `tu::` call. This is a heuristic: it looks for
   `.(fp|ip|gp)\.[a-z_0-9]+\s*=` and `rand\.`. It catches invented-name re-implementations like `dash_enter`.

**Allowlist** (`tools/rust-port/lint_allow.txt`): one glob per line with a reason, for example:
```
rust/melee_engine/src/**            host seams: heap, dvd, pads, audio/render sinks
rust/melee_link/src/**              generated link table
rust/melee/src/{main,cfg,disc,diff,diff_layout,snap,golden*,view,play,netplay,netmatch,np_env,ui,hud_view,ps_view,gx_backend,audio,input,crash,ident,mods,mex,retail/**}.rs   CLI / platform glue
rust/melee_platform/** rust/melee_gx/** rust/melee_audio/** rust/melee_net/** rust/melee_data/** rust/melee_script/**   platform crates (no C game TU)
rust/*/src/tu/gobj.rs rust/melee_runtime/src/gobj.rs   reference model kept as oracle (tests only after step 4)
```
Until step 19, `vs_match*` and the other files listed in step 19 are in `lint_baseline.txt`. They are not on the
allowlist: they are debt, not glue.

**Modes.**
- `--report` (step 3) prints the counts per rule and per crate and exits 0.
- The default mode fails when a violation is not in `lint_baseline.txt` (format `rule<TAB>path<TAB>name`, sorted).
- `--write-baseline` rewrites that file and refuses to add lines unless `--allow-growth` is also passed, which CI never passes.
- `--dedupe-table` emits Appendix A (canonical home and extra bodies), so this note can be regenerated.

**Detection details**, from how Appendix A was measured:
- Definition regex: `^\s*(pub(\(..\))?\s+)?(const\s+)?(unsafe\s+)?(extern "C"\s+)?fn NAME(`.
- Skipped: lines ending `;` (declarations), lines containing `= {` (`ext_fns!` entries), bodies with `todo!(` or
  `unimplemented!(` in the first five lines, and hook forwarders (`match unsafe { HOOK }` / `Some(f) => f(`).

Expected numbers at step 3, before any fix: R2 = 2353 names (3404 bodies). R1 is a subset of those N, V, C and R bodies,
plus the non-tu modules' unique bodies.

---

Step 4 result: `tools/rust-port/link/gen_link.py` parses all 12980 `ext_fns!` entries in `rust/*/src/tu/**` and writes `rust/melee_link/src/gen_table.rs`
(`install()`): 4395 hooks are pointed at the canonical `pub fn NAME` of another tu file through `cast::<_,_,SITE>` (size-checked transmute; a
size mismatch is a build error, and `skip_fix.py` moves those into `link_skip.txt`: 263 entries such as u32 guest address vs native pointer, bool vs i32).
The canonical functions are plain `pub fn` with the C name (the ports are not `#[no_mangle] extern "C"` yet, so this is not the final extern-"C" scheme of 1.2).
Unresolved (`melee_link/unresolved.txt`): nodef 6768 (no tu fn of that C name), sigmismatch 238, ambiguous 28, other 1288 (`$hook` form in ft_0899 and the EXT_HOOK form of melee_modes).
Test `melee_link/tests/hooks.rs`: install() runs, and the trace-reached hooks whose default is todo!() and that stay unlinked (617, `todo_reached_baseline.txt`) must not grow; goal 0.
`install()` is not called by any golden yet, so goldens are unaffected. Three `mod native` in melee_runtime (lobj, pobj, tobj) became `pub`.

Step 5 result: new crate `rust/melee_engine` (seams only; lint-allowed): `gcm.rs` (FST reader with entry numbers; `melee::disc::Disc` now wraps it),
`lowmem.rs` (VirtualAlloc/mmap blocks below 4 GiB), `os.rs` (OSAlloc heaps: 32-byte cells, first fit, coalescing, arena, current heap),
`dvd.rs` (DVDConvertPathToEntrynum/FastOpen/Close and `HSD_DevComRequest` done synchronously; a destination inside the host RAM block is main RAM
whatever the type, because lbFile picks the type by `dst >= 0x80000000`), `archive.rs` (raw image = BE file relocated like `Locate`, native image = gcdecode of
every public the type table knows in one shared graph, native `HSD_Archive` record with LE copies of the tables), `install.rs` (sets the hooks of
initialize/memory/lbheap/lbfile/lbdvd and three new seams in `tu/archive.rs`: `host_parse`, `host_public`, `host_locate_extern`; unset = old behaviour).
`dat_types.rs` is generated by `tools/rust-port/engine/gen_dat_types.py` from `dat_symbols.tsv` (symbol/prefix/suffix -> melee_types record, `*T` pointer root, `raw`);
`--coverage` lists the 70 of 95 call-site symbol literals still without a type; an uncovered public that the game asks for stops and names the symbol.
Test `melee_engine/tests/archive_host.rs` (needs GW_ISO_VANILLA, else vacuous) loads PlCa, PlFx, GrNLa (the task's "GrNFd" is `GrNLa.dat`), ItCo, EfCoData through the real
`lbFile_LoadInto` + `lbArchive_InitializeDAT`, checks raw image = file + Locate (+ externs located to NULL), ftData->x0 and map_head against the `melee_runtime::dat` decode.
Not done: engine-side mods overlay for the DVD seam, ARAM beyond plain copies, wiring `Engine::boot` (step 6). The legacy goldens are unchanged (gate OK).

Step 7 result: `Engine::boot_vs(disc, mem, pads, Scenario)` runs the real `gm_801A4510_init` / `runGameMode_load` / `gm_801A4014_enter` / `gm_801A4D34_begin` from a
`record.sh` scene string (`scenario.rs` answers the `SceneLaunch_*` queries: stage, 4 slots with fighter/human|cpuN, rules, item frequency). StartMelee loads the stage
(GrNLa, GrNBa, GrGr, GrSt boot), items, camera, `Player_InitAllPlayers`, `Fighter_Create` for every slot (PlCo + PlXx + PlXxAJ + costumes), HUD (IfAll) and the effect
data. `melee golden --engine tu [--scenario S] [--frames N] --out CSV` (main.rs `golden_engine`) ticks the engine and writes one NHV1 row per tick through the ported
`RB_NeutralHash` (`Engine::neutral_row`), flushing each frame so a later crash keeps the count. `tools/rust-port/gate.sh --engine tu` compares those CSVs with
`compare_golden.py --ignore-seed` (the hash and seed columns include the particle generators' RNG draws, which are step 10: the legacy run draws 41 values in frame 0
that the engine does not make) and ratchets `engine_progress.tsv` (`ENGINE_FRAMES`, default 200). Frame 0: the fighter words (s0..s11), items and itemsum match the golden
for pad_falcon_fox_fd (64 consecutive frames) and pad_marth_sheik_bf; the CPU scenarios still differ (CPU AI init and the item spawner are step 9/13 work: a fighter word
for the Ice Climbers follower slot is wrongly non-zero and the dreamland items are missing). `melee diff --engine tu` is NOT done (gate skips it for the engine until the first
subsystem migrates; the engine has no `Snapshot` layout yet).

  `ftCo_8009F834` from `ftAction_80073240` at the first Falcon landing, after frame 64); `melee_link/unresolved.txt` + `rust/melee_link/tests/hooks.rs` list all of them.
  Fix a hook by porting the C function in its canonical `tu/` module (or `link_alias.tsv` for a differently spelled hook), then `python tools/rust-port/link/gen_link.py`.
* Step 9 (stage/collision): ground gobjs are made by the real `Ground_801C...` init from `stage_info` (shared static `melee_world::tu::ground::stage_info`), data decoded by
  `melee_engine/src/dat_types.rs` + `dat_custom.rs` (stage DAT arrays: `tools/rust-port/engine/dat_symbols.tsv`, `rust/melee_types/gen/arrays.tsv`). Collision: `mpLib` / `mpColl`.

  hash for pad_falcon_fox_fd). Then drop `--ignore-seed` from the gate.
* Shared state rule (traps.md): a global is one `pub static mut` in its canonical module; ext copies are `pub use`. `tools/rust-port/engine/audit_ext_statics.py` lists the copies
  that are still private zeroed duplicates (split-brain risks, e.g. `gmMainLib_8046B0F0` in the gm* ext blocks); `fix_ext_statics.py NAME CRATE MODULE` repairs one.
* Debugging aids: `MELEE_CRASH_TRACE=1` (vectored handler: fault address plus backtrace), `MELEE_DAT_ISSUES=1` / `MELEE_DAT_LIST=1` (decode issues, public names per archive), the
  `guard` feature of melee_engine (canary allocator, `cargo test -p melee_engine --features guard --test vs_boot`), `--features melee_core/calltrace` for the `ct` sequence.

Step 8 result (pad_falcon_fox_fd 64 -> 269 frames on `--engine tu`, pad_marth_sheik_bf 74 -> 276; both stop at the first `Item_Create` (Fox blaster, Sheik needles: the article data of `ft_data->x48_items` is not decoded, step 13)):
the fighter phase procs (`Fighter_8006A1BC/8006A360/8006ABA0/8006AD10/...`) were already registered and run by `Fighter_Create` + `HSD_GObj_RunProcs`; what stopped them was data and glue. Fixed, each at its cause:
* `ftCo_8009F834` (the effect-command spawner) and `ftCo_800A0098` ported: `melee_fighter/src/tu/ftCo_09f7.rs` (calls `efn::efasync::efAsync_Spawn`, RNG draws x,y,z, fmadds sites). Not C-oracle tested.
* `Fighter_ChangeMotionState`: `fp->x594_s32 = row->x10_animCurrFlags` must be stored in GC byte order into the bitfield bytes (`anon_0_0.bf_0 = ...to_be_bytes()`), as ftwaitanim.rs already did; the native i32 store made `x597_bits` 0 and sent every fighter down the cross-kind animation path.
* Command scripts (`Fighter_WaitAnimData.xC`, lbcommand.c) are RAW streams. `CmdUnion` is now 4 bytes / align 4 (`STREAM_WORD_TYPES` in `gen_types.py`; `Command_05/07.ptr` is a big-endian `u32` guest address, read `u32::from_be(..) as usize`), and a `CmdUnion*` decoded from an archive points into the raw image (`RAW_STREAM_TYPES`). `lbcommand.rs`/`lb_r5.rs` follow. The C oracle `tu_lbcommand` walks native 8-byte unions and is `#[ignore]`d; `tests/lbcommand_raw.rs` covers the raw stream.
* Decode shapes (`rust/melee_types/gen/arrays.tsv`, `gen_types.py`): `ftData.x24/x28` wait tables (`until=0xFFFFFFFF`; `x24` is typed `WaitStruct*`; the union decodes member `i`, `UNION_LIVE_MEMBER`), `ftData_x1C.x8` (`null`), `ftData_x20.x0` (3 entries, entries 0/1 may be NULL), `ftDynamics.x10` (`tab[10]`). A `T**` that the C indexes decoded as ONE element reads garbage past it.
* `ftData_CharacterStateTables[kind]` installed from the retail_ms tables (`melee_link/src/manual.rs`); `MPLIB_8004D164_LINE_COUNT` (the generator cast a `MapCollData*` to `Option<i32>`), `db_804D4AF8` is one shared static, `STALE_DECREMENTS` falls back to `Fighter_804D6548`.
* Link: aliases for `efLib_DestroyAll` (efn::eflib), `HSD_JObjWalkTree` (new pointer-native `jobj_ptr::HSD_JObjWalkTree_ptr`), `efSync_Spawn` (`variadicw` -> `efsync::efSync_Spawn_words`; `gen_link.py` now passes a slice-typed tail as words and drops the result of unit hooks); `melee_chara::adapt::efAsync_Spawn_*` use `efn::efasync` (the old `NativeEfHost` copy crashes on `HSD_ObjAlloc`); `ext_real()` in `seams.rs` answers `__frsqrte` / `__fres` for the by-name `ext_fns!` fallback.
* Engine golden: `melee golden --engine tu` feeds the scenario's pad script (`vs_match::input::pad_fixed`) through `Engine::set_pad_feed` into `PADRead` (row k reads sample k-1: the C harness's one-frame latency). The Ready/GO HUD proc runs on the engine and unlocks input at tick 84 as in the C.
* Debug aids: `MELEE_NH_DUMP=<slot>` prints the 21 inputs of the neutral fighter word per frame on both drivers (diff the engine against `--engine legacy`); `MELEE_PAD_DBG=1` prints the pad feed and `HSD_PadGameStatus`.
* Traps: the engine result depended on the process environment size until the table fixes above (reads past a 1-element decoded array = uninitialised memory); when a run behaves differently with an extra env var, look for a short decoded array. Samus (kind 13) and Master Hand have no `Init_CostumeStrings` in melee_chara, so `cpu_peach_samus_ys_items` stops in `Player_80031CB0`; `cpu_fox_falco_dl` stops in the Greens stage init (`ityaku` item spawn, steps 9/13). Calltrace seqdiff against the C trace is still not run (the C trace build is unfinished, step 1).

Step 9 result (stage and collision procs on the engine, 2026-10-31, branch port/cons-stage):
* The ground gobjs, the `grLast` / `grBattle` procs and `mpLib` / `mpColl` already ran through the real `Ground_*` init and `HSD_GObj_RunProcs` after step 7 (calltrace of
  `pad_falcon_fox_fd` ticks 0..73: `Ground_801C1D84/1CD0/1D38/461C`, `Ground_UpdateMapColl`, `mpLib_80055E9C`, `mpJointUpdateDynamics`, `mpIsland_*`, `grLast_8021A914..B28C`,
  `mpColl_8004730C` / `mpColl_80043754` / `mpColl_80046904` with the line checks). `stage_anim()` / `stage.rs` are not on the engine path (the engine never links them): nothing to retire there.
* Found by comparing the engine's `Ground.u.last` (the Final Destination sway state, map gobj 7) with the legacy `last_bg` at frames 0, 30 and 64: the sway rates `xD4`/`xD8` were wrong because
  (1) `melee_runtime::tu::globals::rand()` returned a PRIVATE unseeded `HsdRand` (split-brain with `random::global::state()`, which the engine seeds with 12345 and `HSD_RandSeedPtr` points at);
  `grlast.rs` and about 60 other `hook_or!(... globals::rand())` sites read it. `globals::rand()` now returns `random::global::state()` (shared engine-code change, one function).
  (2) the C boot makes one `HSD_Randi` before any stage code (`gmMainLib_8015F600(1, 0)` on a fresh save: `Toy_80305058(2, 0x63, 0, 100.0f)`); the engine skipped the card scene, so `boot_vs` makes that
  one draw (`random::global::HSD_Rand()`; the trophy tables are not loaded on the engine path, so the real call would panic in `lbarchive` -- the selected id is not hashed).
  After both, `xC4..xDC` equal the legacy (= C) values bit for bit at frames 0, 30 and 64 (the seed column of the engine run is still off by the step-10 particle draws).
* New engine tests `melee_engine/tests/stage_coll_fd.rs` and `stage_coll_bf.rs` (need the disc, vacuous without; one engine boot per test binary, a second boot in one process crashes, `stage_common/mod.rs`):
  a free `CollData` with a fixed ECB box (`mpColl_8004730C`, no fighter) falls onto the stage StartMelee loaded: FD floor at y 0 (free fall above, stop on the floor, no floor at x 400); BF main floor, side and top
  platforms (landing from above) and free fall between them. They exercise the DAT line/vertex decode, `mpCheckFloor` & co, the step loop and the floor callback on the native stage data.
* Debug aids added to `melee golden --engine tu`: `--calltrace OUT [--calltrace-frames A-B]` (build with `--features melee_core/calltrace`), `MELEE_ENGINE_FT=1` (per-tick motion id / position / velocity / ECB joint
  world positions of slots 0 and 1), `MELEE_ENGINE_DUMP=FRAME:FILE` (slot 0 JObj tree in preorder as `jN rot x y z w trans x y z`, the Ground user data of the map gobjs, and both Fighter records as `p1 fp.NAME HEX`
  in the legacy `--dump-state` field order, so `diff <(grep '^p1 fp' legacy.txt) <(grep '^p1 fp' engine.txt)` is a field diff against the golden-exact legacy state; legacy `melee golden --frames N --dump-state N:FILE`).
* Why the count stays 64 (pad_falcon_fox_fd) / 74 (marth_sheik_bf): the first divergence is NOT stage or collision. At tick 64 Falcon leaves Entry for Fall (`msid 29`); the position of the engine and of the
  legacy are identical before collision (`self_vel`, `prev_pos`, `x28_vec`), but the six ECB joints differ: the engine's fighter JObj tree has 54 of 63 joints with different ROTATIONS (translations equal; legacy
  `j5 rot 3f10ac00 be066000 bf11b800` vs engine `00000000 3c23d70a 00000000`), only `j1..j4` and the root pose agree. So `desired_ecb` is top 15.81 / bottom 1.70 instead of the C's 10.78 / 1.99,
  `mpColl_80043754` makes 3 steps instead of the C's 4 (`x` 14.38 vs > 18) and `cur_pos.y` ends at `0x411ECC68` instead of `0x411ECC66` (two ulp, the whole s0 word). That is the Fall pose after
  `Fighter_ChangeMotionState` (`ftAnim_8006EBE8` / `ftAnim_8006FCE4`, AJ figatree decode): step 8's territory, left to port/cons-fighter. After the Falcon landing at tick 76 the engine panics in
  `lbAnim_8001E7E8` -> `fn_8001E60C` (`lbanim.rs:109`, write to NULL), also anim data. Step 9 re-measure once step 8 merges: the landing needs `mpColl_80046904`'s floor hit with the real ECB (the new tests cover the
  collision half), then `ftCo_Landing_Enter` (`ftAction_80073240` -> `ftCo_8009F834` hook).
* Gate: legacy goldens 1801 / 1801 / 370 / 120 unchanged, engine counts 64 / 74 / 0 / 0 unchanged (no drop), baselines 144, lint OK.

Step 10 result (worktree `C:\melee-wt\cons-effects`, branch `port/cons-effects`: the E: drive was full, 0.16 GB free, so the worktree and its cargo target live on C:):
the particle system and the effects run from their GObjs on the engine, and hash + seed match the C build for every frame the engine reaches:
pad_falcon_fox_fd 64 frames, pad_marth_sheik_bf 74 (the same counts as without effects; both stop in step 8's code, the first Falcon landing /
`lbAnim_8001E7E8` and the first Marth/Sheik fighter state). The 6000-odd `HSD_Rand` draws of those frames are the C's, in the C's order and frames.
* `tu/generator.rs` and `tu/psappsrt.rs` (melee_runtime) are pointer-native ports on the real `HSD_Generator` / `HSD_psAppSRT` records and the real list
  (`hsd_804D78FC`, the `hsd_804D78F8` cursor, `hsd_804D78F4` as a pointer; the C keeps pointers in `u32` globals). Fused sites are the ones the owned-model port
  `crate::generator` (still the oracle, `tests/oracle_generator.rs`) marks. `psInitAppSRT(0, 0xA4)` pools `sizeof(native HSD_psAppSRT)`. `particle.rs` ext defaults call
  the new ports; `hsd_804D78E8/EC/F4` are typed pointers; one `hsd_804D78FC` (script_game / grlib aliases).
* Particle banks. The cmd / tex banks are blobs of GC records addressed by 32-bit offsets from the bank start (NOT archive relocations; `psInitDataBankLocate` adds the base).
  `melee_engine/src/dat_custom.rs` rebuilds each into one native image (`ptcl_cmd`, `ptcl_tex`; `custom:ptclcmd` / `ptcltex` for the stage `map_ptcl` / `map_texg`,
  `custom:efdat` for `effCommonDataTable` and every `eff*DataTable`: two bank pointers, then the native `EF_EffectDesc` array contiguous from byte 16, which is where
  `efAsync_LoadSync` keeps `&entry->data`). Slots hold byte offsets from the image start and `psInitDataBankLocate` relocates them as the C does; texture / palette slots
  hold `raw image address - image base` (wrapping). Version `0x40..0x43` banks: `num` is the index base (generator ids run from `num`, the list pointer is `3 - num` words before
  the table), `count` the records. The command STREAM stays raw big-endian bytes: `particle::CMD_STREAM_BIG_ENDIAN` (set by the engine) makes `psReadFloat` / the bytecode
  read floats MSB first; the synthetic oracle streams stay native-endian. Not handled: form banks (`psNumCmdList`, the callers pass null).
* One eflib/efasync/efdata/efsync/efalt: `tu/efn/*` (c2rs.py ports with the C state oracle `tests/tu_ef.rs`) won. The hand `tu/{eflib,efasync,efdata,efsync,efalt}.rs` and their

Step 11 result (pad_falcon_fox_fd 269 -> 1801 and pad_marth_sheik_bf 276 -> 1801 on `--engine tu`, hash and seed identical to the golden for every frame; legacy counts unchanged 1801 / 1801 / 370 / 120, lint 9444 baselined, no new violation):
what stopped the engine was not camera, HUD or sound but the item path the two scenarios take (Fox blaster, Sheik needles), so the step became "items run on the engine" for these fighters. Each fix at its cause:
* Fighter article tables. `ftData.x48_items` is a `T**` the C indexes by item slot: `dat_custom::ftdata` decodes it as a table of `Article*` sized to the next boundary (`rtable_articles`, also for the `itPublicData` tables).
* Article `x4_specialAttributes` is a `void*` into the big-endian image and the item code reads it as the kind's `s32` / `f32` struct. `dat_custom::fix_articles` (run by `archive.rs` right after `GcDecoder::finish`, because an object's fields exist only after the decoder ran) rebuilds each block (up to the next boundary) native with 32-bit words swapped; shared blocks share one copy. Symptom without it: the Fox laser's `lifetime` read as 4e-41 and the shot died the frame it spawned.

* Split-brain statics, all removed (a private zeroed copy is read as 0 while the owner is written): `melee_modes::tu::globals::stage_info` (fn_8016719C read `unk8C.b5 == 0` on Final Destination, so `unk4E` was overwritten, `x2135` became 0 and the Rebirth collision callback never ran: a 1 ulp spawn height), `HSD_PadMasterStatus` / `HSD_PadCopyStatus` / `HSD_VIData` / `__OSBusClock` / `CostumeListsForeachCharacter` / `if_Scene_Prize_EnterData`, the `HSD_GObj_*Kind` and link-head copies in the world / modes `tu_ext.rs`, `item.rs`'s `it_mobj` (a `[u8; 64]` class record, so `hsdChangeClass` found no `info_init`; `it_mobj_static_init()` now runs at engine boot). `/tmp`-style audit: any `pub static mut X` outside an `ext` block in two files is a candidate (script in the session scratchpad, not committed; `audit_ext_statics.py` only sees the ext copies).

## Round 1 (2026-10-31): the engine runs the retail draw callbacks

- The C scene loop renders every frame, and the render pass has simulation effects: `HSD_JObjSetupMatrix` through the draw callbacks
  rebuilds the world matrices that the next frame's logic reads (`jobj->mtx` in the dynamic-bone reset, hurt capsules, `ftCo_8009CB40`),
  and the `x8B0` part animations. With `HEADLESS_RENDER` the engine skipped it and cpu_fox_falco_dl stopped at frame 142. It now runs
  the draw callbacks (set `MELEE_ENGINE_HEADLESS=1` for the old behaviour): GX / display callees that no hook covers answer zero in
  render-stub mode (`melee_core::ct::RENDER_STUB`, macro `melee_core::unported!`, replaces the `panic!("TODO ... not ported")` arms of the
  `ext_fns!` macros; outside the render pass they still panic). The render pass uses the private render seed (`HSD_RandRenderBegin/End`),
  and the golden hash is taken in `Script_FramePost` (after the logic procs, before the render), like the C build.
- The m-ex dispatch seams (`MEX_ON_KNOCKBACK_ENTER/EXIT`, `ITEM_INVISIBLE/VISIBLE`, `ITEM_DROP/DROP_EXT/PICKUP2`, `REMOVE/APPLY_HEAD_ITEM`,
  `ACTION_STATE_CHANGE`, `MODEL_RENDER`) were "feature off" no-ops. With no override registered `gw_Mex_*Dispatch` runs the vanilla per-kind
  table entry; the engine seams now do (`melee_engine/src/seams.rs`; `MELEE_MEX_OFF=NAME,..` disables single ones for bisecting). Fox's and
  Falco's knockback hand pose (`ftFx_Init_OnKnockbackEnter`) was missing before.
- Tooling: the C call-trace build works again (`gw_calltrace.c` had literal newlines in three format strings); `seqdiff.py --anchor FN
  [--auto-ignore N]` re-chunks both sides at a function (the C log frame starts after the render, the engine frame at the pad renew),
  `countdiff.py` compares per-chunk call COUNTS and ignores names that differ in known-good frames (inlining noise). Traps: the C build logs
  functions before inlining (inlined callees appear only in C), the engine draws no frame-boundary equal to C's, and only functions in `tu/`
  are instrumented (`ftcommon2`, `ftcommon3`, `chars/` bodies are invisible to the trace).
- `melee golden --engine tu` for the pad scenarios needs `MELEE_ENGINE_PRE_DRAWS=1` (tools/rust-port/engine/pre_draws.tsv); the CPU
  scenarios need 0 and compare the seed column too.

- Step 15 continued (same day): cpu_peach_samus_ys_items 559 on the engine (see STATUS.md B). Items on the engine need three data fixes the pad

## Round 3 (2026-11-01): step 16, cpu goldens on the engine

Frame counts on `--engine tu`: pad_falcon_fox_fd 1801, pad_marth_sheik_bf 1801, cpu_fox_falco_dl 3601 (1197 before; the whole golden, seed column
included), cpu_peach_samus_ys_items 1562 (559 before). The gate now runs 3601 frames (`ENGINE_FRAMES`). Method that found everything below:
`melee diff --engine tu` now captures at `Script_FramePost` (the C snapshot point, before the render pass) and also compares the blend-source pose
joints (`parts[i].x4_jobj2`, with the AObj / first FObj state); then the C trace build (`_build/agents/spine-r1-trace`, `pc/platform/gw_snap.c` had
a throw-away `dbg_jobj_out` logger, `src/...` printed the arguments of one function per run, reverted afterwards) against `eprintln!` in the same
function on the Rust side. `MELEE_CT_LIVE=1 MELEE_CT_LIVE_FROM=<frame>` (calltrace build) prints every entry from a frame on: the last lines name the
function a segfault happens in (the crash handler cannot walk the stack of an access violation in release).

Fixes (each was a real divergence from the C):

  `View_AspectBits` / `View_ProjectionScaleBits` answer 4/3 and 1.0 (logic reads them: `ifMagnify_802FB73C` edge); `GXProject` is the gx shim's pure

  `Det_SetInRender`): outside it they panic, which is how the next fixes were found.
- `HSD_MkRotationMtx` and the other pointer-shaped `mtx.c` functions (`tu/mtx.rs`, the owned-model wrappers are `*_owned`): an unlinked hook returned
  zeros and the foot IK produced a gimbal-lock pose.
- `ftData_80085FD4` returns a GC-layout motion row; the hooks that typed it `ftData_80085FD4_ret` read the native layout of that struct (loop flag of
  part animations, `AppealS` side, `SquatWait`, `DownSpot`, `AttackS3/S4`). `ftCo_AppealS` / `SquatWait` / `FTDATA_80085FD4_X8` were linked to the
  pointer-returning function by the generator (non-null is always true): `link_skip.txt` + `melee_link::manual`.
- Retail motion-state tables: `ftCo_DamageFall_Phys/Anim` were re-exports (not found by `gen_retail_ms.py`: no physics in that state); 21 chars
  callbacks took `&mut Fighter` but the table casts the GObj (Peach `SpecialAirSJump_Phys`, Ness / Zelda / Seak / Yoshi / Donkey / Luigi / Mewtwo

## Round 4 (2026-11-01): step 17, engine snapshot and rollback

`melee synctest --engine tu [--scenario S] [--depth K] [--frames N] [--golden CSV]` (`netmatch::engine_game`): `EngineGame` implements `RbGame` over the
real `Engine`; `melee_net::session::SyncTest` saves, ticks, rolls back K frames and resimulates, comparing the OS-heap hash and the NHV1 hash. pad_falcon_fox_fd:
1800 frames, depth 12, 21468 hash checks, 0 mismatches, every golden hash equal (2.5 min). pad_marth_sheik_bf, cpu_fox_falco_dl and cpu_peach: 400 frames depth 6, clean.
How (no per-field state list, nothing to keep in sync):
- `melee_engine::arena`: while the engine runs (`GameScope`, in `boot_vs` and `tick`) the binary's `#[global_allocator]` serves Rust allocations from one
  reserved 16 GiB range (fine size classes, free lists in statics, write-watched). The harness (SyncTest, snapshots, strings) stays on the system heap,
  so a restore cannot clobber it. The engine's arena is ~4.8 GB after boot (the decoded archives are tens of millions of small objects).
- `melee_engine::snapshot`: the state is three kinds of range: the arena, the OS-heap block (`lowmem`, `MEM_WRITE_WATCH`) and the executable's writable PE
  sections (every static). `Base::capture` copies them once; `save` stores only pages that differ from the base (candidates from `GetWriteWatch` for the two big ranges),
  `load` rewrites only dirty + stored pages and zeroes the arena the discarded timeline bumped into. `Engine::frames/ended` are saved by `EngineGame`.
- Traps: (1) thread-locals are outside the statics: `lobj.rs`'s class / light state was `thread_local!`, now a process static (`Gl`, thread-local under `cfg(test)`);
  any new `thread_local!` holding game state breaks rollback. (2) std's HashMap seeds (a thread-local) make arena bytes differ after a replay, so the full-state hash is
  the OS-heap block only; the curated NHV1 hash covers the rest. (3) Blocks over alignment 4096 or 16 GiB fall back to the system heap and are NOT rolled back
  (`arena::NMISSED` counts them; zero in all four scenarios). (4) A snapshot is a page delta against the first one; memory is the base (~5 GB) + dirty pages per snapshot.
- `melee synctest --engine tu --fidelity N` runs N frames then prints the pages where tick, load, tick differ (debug aid), `MELEE_SNAP_DBG=1` prints the ranges.
- Not done: `--from-snapshot` (C snapshot overlay) on the engine, `--dump-state` stays `MELEE_ENGINE_DUMP`; engine `--features guard` allocator test still uses its own allocator.

Step 18 (cutover, partial): `main::use_engine` decides. `--engine legacy` selects the model; so does a flag only the model has (`--mods --slp --gdlp --from-snapshot --dump-state --audio --idle-pads --start`)
or a scenario the engine is not wired for (`pad_falcon_wolf_fd`, `cpu_fox_falco_fd`); everything else runs the engine. `melee golden` without `--frames` runs the golden's length (pad 1802,
cpu 3601) and the engine reads the seed pre-draws from `tools/rust-port/engine/pre_draws.tsv` itself. `melee diff` uses the engine unless `--first` or `--engine legacy` (the bisect needs the model).
gate.sh's legacy branch passes `--engine legacy`; the engine branch also runs a 240-frame synctest. Counts on the engine vs the model: 1801/1801, 1801/1801, 3601/370, 1562/120.
Not done: `view-golden` / `play` / `netplay` on the engine. view.rs / play.rs / netplay.rs hold a `VsMatch` and draw its Player structs through their own scene builders;
the engine path renders by running the retail draw callbacks, whose 374 GX seams (`seams_gen.rs`) answer nothing today. The viewers need those seams to feed `melee_gx::shim`
(a render step, not a sim step), and visual checks are user-only. Until that lands step 19 (delete vs_match) cannot start. Blocker C (cpu_peach 1562) was looked at again for 20 minutes:
at the first differing call the C continues past `Item_802696CC` (`it_80273484`...) and the engine does not, and `it_802750F8` (`xDCC_flag.b3 = 0; Item_802697D4; Item_80269978; b3 = 1`, it_2725.c)
writes `b3 = 1` through `item` after `Item_80269978` may have destroyed it: check whether the engine destroys that item (collided callback) where the C does not.

Step 18, second half (the viewers): the render pass now draws. `melee_engine::gxbridge` (hand-written, one function per GX call the TUs make) forwards to `melee_gx::shim::GxShim`; the
install table `gxbridge_gen.rs` is generated by `tools/rust-port/engine/gen_gx_bridge.py` from the bridge's own signatures and the hook list in `seams_gen.rs` (329 hooks over all TUs). It is
installed only when `melee_engine::engine::RENDER` is set before boot (the viewers and `--shots`; the goldens stay silent and fast). Notes: (1) the game's `GXEnd` is a macro that vanished in
the port: the bridge ends the open primitive before any other GX call; (2) the engine's VI state has no XFBs, so `HSD_VICopyXFBAsync` returns before `GXCopyDisp`; `Engine::tick` calls
`gxbridge::finish_frame()` instead; (3) texture / TLUT / light objects are kept in side tables keyed by the address of the game's record; image, TLUT and vertex-array bytes are copied once per
(address, length) into `GxData` (the extent comes from the archive image or heap block holding the pointer). What it shows (checked by eye on PNGs, not an oracle): the real stage, camera, fighters
with their costumes, nametags, the READY / GO HUD, effects (fire trails, shield bubble, Aurora), score text. Known: the fighters' first frames look washed out, lights are loaded without
specular, nothing is checked against a C screenshot.
- `melee golden --engine tu --shots 30,60 [--shot-dir D]` renders offscreen (`melee_gx::render::Renderer`, no window) to `engine_<scenario>_<frame>.png`.
- `melee view-golden` (`engine_view.rs`): the same in the window (`Presenter`), `--shots`, `--frames`. The window is created unfocused when `MELEE_WINDOW_UNFOCUSED` is set.
- `melee netplay` (`NetGame` trait in netmatch.rs, `EngineGame` for the engine): two local peers with `--seed 12345`: both 1801/1801 equal to the C golden through real rollbacks (depth up to 7), no
  session or transport desyncs. `--live-rng` sets the seed per frame from the agreed seed. Not done: stage other than FD / BF, the lobby's stock rule (the engine runs the golden's time rule).
- `melee play` (play.rs `EngMatch`): the stand-in menus stay, the match is the engine (pause and the START handling are the game's own, L+R+A+START still quits at the pad layer). One engine match
  per process (the host and the TU statics boot once: a second match toasts "RESTART"); `--mods` or `--engine legacy` selects the model. `--match falcon,fox,GrNLa.dat` skips the menus (a hands-off test).
  Audio in an engine match is the menu audio only (the game's `lbAudioAx` calls are a no-op seam); results come from `Player_GetFalls` and the fighters' percent.
- Memory: the engine's arena is ~4.5 GB (the decoded archives: ~20M 144-byte records, ~2M 128/96-byte ones, two 400 MB buffers); `snapshot::Base` keeps the arena's base copy-on-first-write (`cow.rs`, a vectored
  exception handler) so a rollback session costs the dirty pages, not a second 4.5 GB. Two netplay peers on one machine need ~10 GB.
- Blocks step 19: the legacy model is still the only implementation of `--slp` / `--gdlp` replays, `--mods` (the engine has no mods overlay on its DVD seam), the `--audio` proof, `--from-snapshot` and
  `melee diff --first`; the retail-menu stand-ins in `play` use `vs_match::input`. Deleting `vs_match` means giving those four an engine version first.

Step 19 prerequisites (round 4, still not the deletion): the model-only features now run on the engine.
- `--slp` / `--gdlp` replays: `golden_engine` routes the fighter code's `Replay_*` platform hooks (`melee_fighter::platform::hook`) to `melee_data::Player` and forces the seed per frame;
  `tools/rust-port/golden/check_slp.sh` (falcon v fox .slp and .gdlp) is equal to the C playback (1801 / 1802 frames). The marth_sheik .slp has never been in the check (golden count 0 on both paths).
- `--mods DIR`: `Gcm::set_overlay` (melee_engine): a path the mods answer keeps its disc entry number and its bytes come from the mod (`engine_disc`, `set_engine_mods`; `play --mods` too).
  Files a mod ADDS (new fighters, m-ex) have no disc entry and are not served: m-ex needs the engine's extension step.

  `engine_audio_gen.rs`, 177 hooks, `tools/rust-port/engine/gen_audio_bridge.py`) to `melee_audio::sim`, after the link table (`melee_engine::engine::POST_LINK`). The real scene code makes the calls (announcer,

  (`MELEE_ENGINE_HEADLESS`, like the C's turbo capture: the draw callbacks have sound side effects, the off-screen arrow's). Result: pad_falcon_fox_fd 1800 frames = 6106 sub-frames, mix hash
  0xf4956676e79a4037 = the C capture (in the gate now), pad_marth_sheik_bf 843 sub-frames equal (0x4071ad16fd1751fd). Open: `--pause 300:420` gives the right length (702 ticks) but not the C mix
  (the engine misses the duck `UAV 44` calls at the pause and resumes 11 ticks after pausing: the pause input path), and `--time-secs 60` stops at `melee_runtime::tu::rumble` (PADControlMotor unported seam).
  Bugs the audio proof found in the engine's data path: `ftAction_80071B50` read big-endian sfx ids natively (`u32::from_be` now), `_FtSFXArr.sfx_ids` had no length (`arrays.tsv`), the pad feed reported ports 3 and 4 as connected.

## Round 5 (2026-11-01): steps 20 to 22 as a mechanical fold

Tools (tools/rust-port/dedupe/, all idempotent, all end with a `cargo build` + `fix2`-style import repair by hand):
- `fold_reexports.py --crate rust/melee_chara --prefix chars`: a tu file that re-exports `pub use crate::chars::rN::<Char>::<tu>::{names}` gets the bodies (and the private helpers they use) moved in, the old block keeps `pub use crate::tu::<file>::{names}` so tests (`tests/chars_ref.rs`, `chars_dispatch.rs`) and `melee_link` keep resolving. 1418 bodies.
- `fold_blocks.py`: the remaining `pub mod <c stem> { pub fn .. }` blocks of the hand-written chars modules; a name the tu file already has (the gobj callback) is moved as `<name>_fp` (the typed `&mut Fighter` variant) with `pub use .. as <old>` left behind. Creates `tu/<stem>.rs` for the Crazy Hand / Purin TUs that had none.
- `fold_names.py --crate rust/<crate>`: every `fn` (top level, or inside a `mod <c stem>` block) of a non-tu, non-allowlisted file whose name is a C-defined function moves to `tu/<c stem>.rs` (found under `tu/` recursively, so `tu/efn/*` re-exports are respected; created when missing). The source keeps `pub use crate::tu::<stem>::<name> as <old>;`; its private items become `pub(crate)`; imports are copied by name, and any name the destination already has (defined, imported or used) is bound under an alias `<name>_<srcmod>` so the moved body never silently picks up the destination's item.
- `insert_ct.py` afterwards adds `ct!` to the moved bodies (so the call trace now sees them).
Results (logic lint): R1 3856 -> 265, R2 4652 -> 3386, baseline 9225 -> 4439 entries; engine frames unchanged (1801 / 1801 / 3601 / 1652), synctest and audio equal. Unit tests: the set of failing test targets of melee_chara / melee_fighter / melee_items / melee_world equals HEAD~ (a third of the tu_*_vs_c targets and chars_ref fail at HEAD too: `_fp` names in the dispatch table are stale, see STATUS.md); melee_modes / melee_runtime tests were not compared (compile only).
Not done: deleting the typed `_fp` / `*_ref` variants (two bodies per function remain where a gobj c2rs body and an oracle-checked typed body both exist), `adapt.rs` / `stubs.rs` (the `port_hook!` defaults of melee_chara), `hooks.rs` of melee_fighter (seams + doc comments in a macro; the fold script breaks it, skipped), the 3386 R2 left are mostly step 23 (TU-to-TU local copies) and static-name collisions.

## Round 8 (2026-11-01): blocker C solved, all four goldens complete on the engine

cpu_peach_samus_ys_items 1652 -> 3601 (the whole golden, seed column included; the gate used to ignore the seed for the CPU scenarios, which hid a
divergence that starts 3 frames before the first hash difference; compare with `compare_golden.py` without `--ignore-seed` to see the real count).
Method: C call sequence of the failing frame (`_build/agents/<name>`, trace exe from `tools/rust-port/trace/build_trace.sh`, rebuilt per TU in 30 s with
`--tu`), Rust call trace (`--features melee_core/calltrace`, separate `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`), `countdiff.py` / `seqdiff.py`, `MELEE_RAND_TRACE` on both sides
(per-frame draw callers), then a temporary `OSReport` in the C TU and an `eprintln!` in the Rust body for the one function the trace named. Each fix:
- `it_8026B924` (returns `s32`) was declared `f32` in `ftcpuattack.rs` ext_fns; `it_8026B560/574` (return `float`) were declared `i32` in `ftlib.rs`.
  `melee_link::cast` transmuted same-size values, so the wrong ABI class was silent: it now panics on a float/int mismatch (site id in the message).
- `lbColl_800077A0`: the C passes `sqrt_tmp - 1` (outside the array) as the volatile spill slot of its second `sqrtf_store`; clang treats the store as UB and the
  shipping build returns the argument unchanged (measured: `sq == n1`). The Rust takes no sqrt there. `mpLib_80056C54` has the same pattern (`sqrt_tmp - 4/5`);
  the Rust takes a real sqrt, not exercised by any golden: re-measure before trusting it.
- Native-vs-GC layout traps (all of the same family: a C struct overlay, or a union arm, that depends on 4-byte pointer slots):
  Kinoko scale scratch pair (`walk.middle/fast_anim_frame`) overlapped the saved velocities of `fn_800D2B30/BF8`; it lives in a static per fighter now;
  `ftColl_8007A06C` writes its result over `&fp->dmg.x1870` (native `x1870` is an 8-byte pointer): built locally, stored field by field;
  Beam Sword attributes (`itSword_UnkArticle1` has `UNK_T` fields before `xC`) are read at their GC offsets;
  Shy Guy `attr[0]` is a relocated pointer to its health (`dat_custom::HEIHO_HEALTH` stores the value in the swapped copy).
- Hooks the engine never installed: `GROUND_FRICTION_MUL` and `PLAYER_UPDATE_JOYSTICK_COUNT` (melee_fighter/hooks.rs defaults are the legacy ports),
  the three `ftCommon_UpdateFacing` seams ("ambiguous" in `unresolved.txt`), the `it_80285300` ext of ftafterimage (cast a GObj to an `Item`).
  `melee_link/unresolved.txt` still lists 2500 unresolved hooks: any of them panics when first reached.
- Debug aid: `MELEE_WATCH_FIGHTER=slot,field,fromframe` (fields gr_vel, motion_id, xE4, xE8, mv24, mv28) with the calltrace build reports the function entry after
  which the u32 changed (`MELEE_CT_LIVE=1 MELEE_CT_LIVE_FROM=f` lists the entries before it).
Not done: the `*_vs_c` oracle tests of `itheiho` (the attr pointer) and `lbcollision` were not re-run beyond `tu_lbcollision`; step 23 (lint baseline 986) and 24 are untouched this round.

Lint (step 23): `lint_logic.py` now treats a C name defined in several C files (`stageGObj1_OnInit`, `doEnter`, `setupStageCallbacks`, ...) as one function
per defining file: a tu body in a file named after one of the defining C stems is that file's own body, not a copy (R2 760 -> 512); `main` is not game logic.
Baseline 986 -> 736 entries, no Rust change. What is left in R2 is the pointer-based `*_native` / `jobj_ptr` / `jobj_world` / `gobj_native` / `state_native` /
`tev_native` / `displayfunc_native` bodies next to the owned-model ones (about 140), the `gm*` typed copies (melee_modes, about 160) and the 105 melee_fighter
duplicates; step 24 needs all of them at zero. Oracle tests that now differ on purpose: `kinds_itsword` op 9, `oracle_round13` (R13_80285300) and `tu_itheiho`
compare against a natively compiled (64-bit) C oracle, whose `itSword_UnkArticle1` / Shy Guy attribute layout differs from the shipping 32-bit build the engine
now follows.

## Round 11 (2026-11-01): step 23 continued, canonical-copy decisions, blocker E check

Decisions by evidence (which copy matches its C oracle and is what the engine runs):
- `ef::eflib` (melee_world/src/ef/eflib.rs, a model taking the plink heads as a slice; 3 fns) vs `tu::efn::eflib`: the model was used only by its own test; the engine
  links `tu::efn::eflib` (reads `gobj_native::HSD_GObjPLinkHead`). Kept `tu::efn`, deleted the model, and re-pointed the C oracle `tests/eflib_r3_vs_c.rs` (3000 random list pairs) at
  `efLib_SetFlags / PauseAll / ResumeAll` of `tu::efn::eflib` (it sets `ext::HSD_GObjPLinkHead`): green, so the engine copy is now the one checked against the C.
  `ef/mod.rs` stays as an empty module (path kept for `melee_world::ef`).
- `tu::jobj_world` / `jobj_ptr` / `tev_native` (raw-pointer, C layout) vs `tu::jobj` / `tu::tev` (arena `JObjTree`, owned `HsdRender`): the pointer copies are the ones the engine
  and the fighter/world/item TUs call, and they have the C oracles (`pure_jobj_world`, `tu_tev_native`, `jobjptr_vs_c`). The arena/`HsdRender` copies are only reached by the retail
  menu stand-ins (`rust/melee/src/retail/`, `gx_backend.rs`) and the `state.rs` model. So the pointer copies are the keepers, but the arena ones cannot be deleted until those stand-ins
  go (the task said not to port kit menus), and the lint's canonical choice (lowest crate / first file) points the other way for these names. Not forced: the 24 `tu::jobj` and 27 `tu::tev`
  entries stay in the baseline; they are the "pointer-based render model" item.
  Done there: `tev_native` no longer has its own `CompareRGB / CompareRGBA / CopyRGB / HSD_Index2TevStage / HSD_TevStage2Index`; it uses/forwards to `tu::tev` (identical semantics: alpha ignored
  for RGB, stage range 0..16), as it already did for `HSD_Channel2Num` and the `2Num` pair. `tu_tev_native` stays green.

Blocker E check (`melee play` headless, MELEE_PAD_SCRIPT, window unfocused, adapter ignored): new script `tools/rust-port/play/menu_to_results.txt` drives title -> main menu (stick down to
VS) -> VS menu -> CSS -> SSS -> loading -> match -> results on the engine (`play: tick 478 mainmenu -> css`, `774 css -> sss`, `817 sss -> loading`, `820 loading -> match`, `1814 match -> results`,
closed after 1904 ticks, exit 0, no `todo!` panic). The retail main menu is driven by `retail/mainmenu.rs` (the old full_loop.txt presses A on the first item, which is now 1P mode, and stops at
"NOT IN THE NATIVE PORT YET"; use the new script). The direct `todo!` defaults named in blocker E (`lbBgFlash_80021C48`, `efAsync_Spawn`, `Camera_RequestQuake`, `HSD_JObjSetMtxDirtySub` in
`ftCo_Damage` / `ft_0c8c` / `JumpAerial`) are not in `melee_link/unresolved.txt` for melee_fighter any more (the link layer installs them); they stay as ext defaults, not panics, on the
paths a match reaches. Menus other than VS (1P, Toy, Settings, Data) show the toast and do not enter a scene; they were not exercised beyond the toast.
